A ribbon had become a tight bound loop across its axle. The simple cause and effect was visible now; no astrological explanation improved it. Rusk's claim that the motion conferred fortune sounded particularly foolish beside a broken lamp. A handful of guests watched the out-of-control display settle. The room became distinctly quieter.","\"Stand down from the display,\" the technician told him when he tried to restart it.","Mara nearly applauded. The man had taken responsibility for the machine, and the owner could wait. A diplomatic request had failed; a clear instruction worked. The gallery mogul looked miserable for a moment before his art-loving guests began asking whether Della could repair it. Her hard science background had abruptly become fashionable.","\"A lesson you may unlearn tomorrow,\" she murmured to Mara.","She was backed by friends tonight, and she was skilled at working flexibly. Mara could envisage the result without another argument. Copious explanations would not beef up their chances. They needed to navigate the room, not mark time beside a host's ego. His inexorable impatience seemed to intensify when Della refused to expend her energy apologizing. That would appal anyone who mistook obedience for gratitude.","\"Then let them learn it twice.\"","The designer had once aspired to laboratories. She liked textiles better because the results could move with a person. Her deep-rooted interest had been dismissed as frivolous in one profession and insufficiently glamorous in another. Job-hunting had become a rut of explaining the same choice. Tonight she wanted to prove herself through work, not another speech.","Rusk insisted the search continue. The brooch, he said, was in the case of the blue mineral. His assistant corrected him: that phrase appeared nowhere in the clue. He had just revealed more than he intended. Mara turned toward a display beneath a distinctive zigzag bracket. The blue stone's case stood beside an oval portrait, a cylinder of amber glass, and a little pyramid.","Della looked at the arrangement relationally. The star in the original photograph had pointed to the triangle of three objects, not the pool. The damaged display had been a diversion. Their hitherto untested clue was becoming fruitful. Mara would not attempt to open a case without the attendant, but she asked him to check whether the brooch belonged in its inventory.","A small drawer held a biodegradable packet, not the brooch. Inside, an introductory note asked the guest to describe the star as a living thing. The wording perplexed Rusk's followers. Della said it described a sea creature. Her evolutionary work had included structures in marine organisms. The clue's contextual reading did not require a fashion image at all. They worked collaboratively, and Della persistently refused to be hurried.","They moved toward a sculpture of a starfish. Rusk's smile vanished. He ordered his assistant to clamp down on any search beyond the marked route. She showed him the map. The sculpture stood inside the boundary. Mara asked him to stop expending everyone's patience by selectively remembering his own rules. He could leave the judging to the person he had appointed.","The assistant surprised herself by agreeing. She had followed him unthinkingly through too many dinners. She would respond to the printed instruction this time. His authority did not exempt him from it. The guests' growing interest could intensify his embarrassment, but that was not Della's responsibility. She should not have to get out of a fair contest simply to restore his comfort.","Mara bent beside the starfish and found a tiny wrinkle in the cloth beneath it. The rim concealed a shallow pocket. She had to jog back for the attendant before inspecting it; nobody wanted her accused of planting the prize. Cassian followed, shielding her skirt from the low candles. The enormous room could not have produced a more attentive escort. Someone here would learn a lesson about free work.","In desperation, Rusk pointed at a map of the New World, claiming its green land offered the right clue. He was bluffing. Della put a dot beside the actual instruction and set her empty saucer down. The small, calm movement helped drive her point home: she had read his rules. This corner was home to the sculpture they wanted, and they would inspect it. A living standard could depend on one paid order.","The attendant lifted the cloth. A dip in the base held another image, this time a copy of Della's original sketch. Someone had cropped a name from a drawing before placing it there. The clue became damning in a different way: Rusk had planned to present her design as his gallery's exclusive concept. The wager was not merely a party joke. If she lost, the original and the flattering story would both be his.","Della's voice shook. \"My work is not yours because you found a use for it.\"","\"It could have elevated you.\"","\"It could have paid me.\"","The exchange was concise enough to silence the room. Mara held out the original invoice. The wager had promised purchase at that price, not unpaid exposure. She would not go to war with the whole industry tonight, but one host could be held to one promise. His ill-managed performance was leaving him fewer graceful exits.","A new clue asked for a word formed by an approximate line through the starfish's arms. Della solved it flexibly, turning the paper rather than the sculpture. The letters spelled BREAD. Rusk had a big kitchen display full of ceramic food. She laughed. \"You wanted me to bake a star, so you hid it in a loaf.\"","Mara did not need to multiply the theory. They went to the bread. Its hollow center held the brooch in a soft sleeve. The delicate points were unbroken. A crowd pressed forward, threatening to make the narrow aisle out of control again. Cassian held people back while the attendant removed the prize. Their collaborative work required no elaborate title.","Rusk tried a toxic little joke about a woman needing three helpers. Mara smiled. \"You employed twelve to hide it. Shall we compare?\"","The laughter was catastrophic to his dignity. Della stood on the verge of tears, but this time they were joyful. A band across her chest seemed to loosen. She had not cured an industry. She had won her own star back."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1063,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0263\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：The Star He Could Not →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x056c03","title":"Twenty Stars, and One for Her","volume":"婚后外传：The Star He Could Not Keep","anchor":"chapter-0264","paragraphs":["Rusk paid the invoice in front of his guests. The assistant confirmed the order for twenty pieces and recorded the agreed delivery dates. Della took responsibility for fulfilling a realistic schedule, with a deposit before materials were purchased. The job-hunting designer had arrived expecting to be patronized. She left the table with paid work, her original brooch, and several new customers waiting to speak to her. She would attempt to expand only after payment. The contract did not presuppose immediate success.","Mara refused the host's offer of a prize necklace. \"My friend was the contestant.\"","\"You took her place.\"","\"And she found the star.\"","The new studio would be backed by the deposit, not an extravagant loan. Della wanted to beef up her tools only after the first payment. She could navigate a small budget. In the case of the large gallery order, she would leave the display schedule to its manager. The solvable problem had taken her to the verge of a different working life. She no longer needed to mark time in the mogul's corridor. Della brought her excitement under control. She could unlearn unnecessary apologies.","Cassian's smile was big enough to make Rusk look away. The mogul had aspired to a fashionable triumph. His ill-managed wager had produced one, though the heroine was somebody else. The art-loving guests seemed happy with the substitution. They had not come to watch a man clamp down on every person more interesting than himself.","Della's distinctive brooch rested on a black cloth. Its oval center held tiny points that looked different from every angle. A pyramid of clay samples showed how she had learned to bake the design without losing its sharpness. Her hard science training and her love of ornament had worked collaboratively. They did not need to go to war with each other to make her respectable.","A buyer asked whether the star was astrological. \"It can be, if you like that story,\" Della said. \"My own sketch began with a sea creature.\"","The contextual answer pleased Mara. The wearer could enjoy a private meaning without pretending the object proved it. A fashion image did not have to erase the maker's origin. Nor did an expensive room confer taste on every person who entered it.","The magazine photographer asked to crop a face from a portrait for the evening's souvenir cards. Della agreed to her own, after seeing the layout. He described the design as the night's most original piece, then asked whether she liked the wording. She changed one word. The tiny correction felt fruitful in a way no grand apology could have matched. His praise helped elevate her mood without requiring her to multiply her promises.","Mara studied the arrangement relationally, noticing how the star's points answered a zigzag seam on Della's sleeve. The girl had made both. One had hitherto been hidden beneath an apron, the other selectively dismissed as amateur. Together they made an elegant argument without a speech. Rusk's deep-rooted assumption about fashionable bodies had missed the imagination working beside them.","The technician reported that the garden mechanism would stay off until repaired. Its bound ribbons had been removed, but a linked bearing needed inspection. The cause and effect had become clear enough to prevent another restart. The host could not describe a damaged display as safe simply because he wanted the lights moving. His assistant asked him to stand down from further instructions, and he did. The display explained her evolutionary inspiration, and she spoke flexibly about later designs.","A diplomatic assistant brought copious apologies and a plate of food. Rusk's out-of-control pride might appal her privately; she would not repeat his insults. Her increasing assurance seemed damning to his old manner. The out-of-town guests wanted to learn a lesson from the design, and the photographer was skilled at making their interest visible. A stand-alone digital service would host Della's portfolio without pretending to replace her workshop.","Outside, a jog of lanterns marked the path toward the carriage court. The odd line was deliberate, turning around a rose bed rather than cutting through it. Mara followed its dip toward a bench. A wrinkle in her skirt caught beneath her hand, and Cassian gently freed it. \"A useful husband,\" she said.","\"You sound surprised.\"","\"I enjoy fresh evidence.\"","He kissed her until she forgot the reply she had planned. The garden was home to a few guests who pretended not to notice. Della, standing at the door, laughed openly. Mara waved her over before the younger woman could retreat in embarrassment. The profit-making order would let her expend time on experiments again.","\"What next?\" Mara asked.","Della could envisage a small workshop rather than a giant label. A better living standard would mean paying rent without choosing between heat and material. She had persistently tried to prove herself to people who did not want to see her. Now she wanted to work for those who did. The change was not inexorable progress. It was an ordinary decision she could make tomorrow. Della's smile drove her point home. Her joy began to intensify.","\"And tonight?\" Cassian asked.","\"Tonight I eat something.\"","They laughed. The miserable morning had become a story she could bear to tell. In desperation, she had accepted help; the help had not made her smaller. Mara wanted to make that point plain, but Della already seemed to know. She asked the two of them to choose a brooch from the next batch. Cassian immediately selected the largest star for his wife.","\"Toxic favoritism,\" Mara said.","\"An unthinkingly honest response.\"","The answer made her laugh against his shoulder. The formal room behind them was no longer out of control, and its host no longer directed every glance. The assistant had her printed rules, Della had her star, and the technical crew had permission to do the work properly.","Mara rose from the bench and held out her hand. \"Let's get out of here.\"","Cassian took it, more than willing. They left through the little garden, stepping over a rut in the path. At the gate, he turned her once beneath the final lamp. House, Mara was handed a sword, a wedding veil, and a biographical note declaring her a pirate. \"Altogether inaccurate,\" Cassian said. \"She steals more elegantly.\" Minna called elegance an impossibility in that veil.","The retired actors surrounding them cheered. Their old guild's affiliation mattered less than friendship now, though several still claimed fame from the same disastrous film. Noor had arranged the afternoon's reunion. It was not a treatment or a publicity event. The actors wanted a performance, a dinner, and the freedom to be ridiculous with people they trusted. Noor was proficient at organizing chaos, and today her skill was invaluable.","Minna Venn, their hostess, had arthritis and used a cane for persistent lameness. She also had an opinion on everyone's posture. Her colleague Oliver had paralysis after a spinal injury and performed seated. Neither intended to become a symbol of misery. Minna ordered a full-time assistant to stop fussing over her shawl and bring the prop crown. Her esteem for practical help exceeded her patience for pity.","The play concerned a princess in captivity, an explorer's missing paternity letter, and an island facing drought. A dearth of fresh water threatened famine, while deforestation endangered the realm's last trees. Its political metaphor was not subtle. The tyrant promised civilisation and brought repression. He called slavery an employment arrangement, a falsehood the cast enjoyed demolishing aloud.","The small arena contained approximately forty seats and a somewhat muscular papier-mâché whale. Its disintegration had been arrested with tape. The poster from the Revolutionary War play covered its emptiness on one side. Minna said modern paint might outdate the old scenery, but she would not incur a bill to revolutionize a whale. Its flavor was nostalgia. Mara found this profoundly reasonable, if somewhat difficult to explain.","Adela Pan, the youngest actor, was returning after a long absence. She had an anxiety disorder, and her readiness varied from day to day. Parenthood had made rehearsal schedules difficult; depression had made other parts of life harder. Her partner Felix came to help with their daughter, not to supervise her. The couple wanted parity at home as well as onstage. A speech about paternity in the script made him laugh so hard he missed his cue.","\"Its historic citizenship rules are nonsense,\" he said.","\"A plurality of nonsenses,\" Oliver agreed. \"The king believes affiliation is hereditary.\"","Minna's bosom shook with laughter. Her costume had been designed for a much younger woman with less enthusiasm for pudding. She refused a replacement. Its resultant tightness made her royal posture impressively rigid. Cassian offered to loosen the fastener. \"Not until the audience appreciates my sacrifice,\" she said.","The afternoon's constraint was the weather. Moisture had swollen a frame in the outdoor stage, leaving a gap beneath a painted wall. The dearth of good repair timber had delayed its replacement during the recession. Ivo had sent instructions, but the local carpenter would do the work. No one needed Mara's sword near a real structural problem. The frame stayed stationary until checked.","Noor's phone brought a message from an absent actor. He had influenza, which he called flu whenever he did not want to sound dramatic. A fever had kept him home. He would join by audio after resting. The old quarantine poster on the dressing-room wall was a joke from another production, not a current medical instruction. Minna refused to lend it to him. \"You'll use it to demand biscuits.\" The real necessity was a safe repair, not luck. Minna accepted its reasonableness, and her confidence was traceable to the carpenter's careful explanation.","Mara looked at the poster's out-of-date lettering. Its cartoon pest wore a crown. The play had once been sold as an animation, then abandoned when the studio ran out of money. The actors kept the props because their association carried memory. Noor understood the attachment without calling it dependency. A familiar object could matter without becoming a delusion about the past.","The first scene's stand-off involved a self-deceiving king. He called the princess's plight a necessity, then claimed that slavery preserved peace in the realm. Oliver gave the line such poisonous sweetness that the cast booed. Its untrue promise would be easy to demolish. The hardship mattered; the prominence of the villain's speeches should not distort it. Mara wanted his coming defeat to feel satisfying.","Oliver's old costume smelled of soap. He had requested it be cleaned with the rest, and the house's hygiene arrangements were now finally adequate. A rash on an actor's wrist had already been checked privately; it was nobody else's dramatic opportunity. Noor made no public diagnoses. She wanted to dine with friends, not spend the afternoon as a walking clinic.","The script's assimilation story troubled Adela. The princess was supposed to abandon her language to marry the hero. \"Can we change that?\" she asked. They could relax once the mess was cleared. Its extinction as a hazard would expand their freedom.","\"A revolutionary idea,\" Minna said. \"Autonomy. In a romance.\"","They rewrote the scene. The lovers would learn each other's words. Their accord would require effort, not one person's disappearance. The change relieved a small friction that had persisted in the cast for years. Mara admired how a simple question could let an idea take root without a speech about everybody's former mistakes.","The sword was blunt, the veil inflammable only in Minna's opinion, and the setting far too bright for a tragedy. A host of old jokes made the atmosphere lively. The house's pervasive affection did not erase insecurity; it gave people somewhere to sit with it. Adela asked for a short pause, got one without argument, and returned when ready.","Cassian took his wife's hand. \"Your pirate has a proposal.\"","\"Dishonor?\"","\"Dinner after the rescue.\"","\"A question of honour, then.\"","She kissed him. Minna declared their improvised chemistry vulgar and ordered them to save it for the stage. The provocative remark produced a laugh from everyone, including Noor. A gloomy afternoon had become a domain of old rivalries and new play.","At the first cue, a wooden ship rolled out. Its painted mast leaned toward the curtain. The carpenter stopped the rehearsal at once. Mara put down her sword. There would be no pretending a genuine risk was part of the entertainment. The scare was small, but the frame needed attention before luck decided the next scene."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1059,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0265\" House Players","anchor":"chapter-0266","paragraphs":["The carpenter found a loose brace beneath the ship. He could repair it, but not while people stood on the platform. Minna called the delay an impossibility, then immediately helped clear the route. Her impatience belonged to the stage, not a refusal to listen. Cassian carried the light props, while Mara moved a chair beside Oliver's access path. Hygiene checks had included the shared props. Minna tasted the stew and praised its flavor. The whale's disintegration troubled nobody; its plight was comic.","\"Don't unload your whole problem onto the strongest man,\" Minna told the crew.","\"A devastatingly accurate compliment,\" Cassian answered. Their current constraint was moisture in the timber.","The friction dissolved into laughter. The former stars might have been difficult in their realm of fame, but friendship gave them a language for correction. Their guild affiliation had survived recession, a dearth of work, and several spectacular arguments. Liberty House was a domain they had made together. They could defend it without pretending its aging set was sound.","Adela's anxiety disorder made the unexpected pause difficult. She chose a quiet room with Felix, then asked him to stay by the open door rather than beside her chair. Her autonomy included that distinction. Parenthood had accustomed them to negotiating small practical needs. A reference to paternity in the old script had become a running joke, but parity was something they worked at when nobody watched.","The emptiness in the little arena lasted approximately ten minutes. Its flavor changed when the cast returned. Captivity had been misery in the story; rehearsal had become a comic dependency on Minna's constant corrections. She did not conclude that fame justified everything she said. If her opinion was a delusion, Oliver generally told her. Their long friendship could survive an out-of-date costume argument.","Noor checked whether the absent actor wanted to continue the audio call. His influenza had left him tired; he would not sing through a fever to prove commitment. \"Flu has destroyed my career,\" he announced.","\"Your last film tried harder,\" Minna replied.","His laugh ended in a cough, and he chose to leave the call. The cast sent a greeting-card with biscuits drawn on it. They would not deliver a crowd to his door. The old quarantine poster remained in the dressing room, where its cartoon pest presided over the soap and towels.","The repaired scene needed a new entrance. Oliver proposed that the pirate appear beside him instead of leaping onto the platform. His paralysis did not prevent him from redesigning a scene. Minna's arthritis and lameness also altered the blocking; neither forced her out of the story. The change brought a plurality of bodies into the same comic arrangement without making any one of them an educational exhibit.","Mara tried the entrance. The veil caught on her sword, producing chaos altogether unrelated to the carpenter's work. Cassian rescued the fabric, then bowed so low that the false crown slid toward his nose. Minna's bosom heaved with delight. The resultant mess was better than the polished animation the studio had once envisaged. Nobody needed a special effect to enjoy two accomplished people losing a fight with tulle.","\"A scare for the dignity,\" Mara said. No computer would outdate Oliver's comic timing or revolutionize the pleasure of watching it.","\"A traceable cause,\" Noor answered.","The word made Oliver demand a biographical explanation of Cassian's kneeling technique. Noor refused to distort a happy moment into an interview. This was not a cognitive assessment or a commentary on symptoms. The actors could have a comic disaster without somebody extracting a lesson from it.","The play's realm had been rewritten too. Drought and deforestation still threatened famine, but its citizens now organized their own wells instead of waiting for a prince. The threatened extinction of a tree was depicted by children carrying bare branches. The representation was simple enough to remain intelligible from the back row. Its story of civilisation no longer used slavery as a convenient decorative evil; a former captive chose what happened after freedom.","The self-deceiving king's provocative promises had a prominent place in the rewritten scene. His prominence made his failure delicious. Mara delivered the princess's reply accurately: 'A necessity to you is not a necessity to me.' The simplicity let the actors learn to relax into the exchange. Its reasonableness did not reduce its force. A young stagehand said luck had brought her the best afternoon of the month.","The plot's repression survived, or the villain would have nothing to do. He declared that citizenship depended on agreeing with him. The princess called that untrue. The crowd cheered when she kept her own name. Assimilation was no longer presented as the price of love. The lovers' accord was reciprocal, and the cast's new version took root as they rehearsed it. The heroine's hardship had left an audience ready to applaud her liberty.","Adela returned for the short scene she had chosen. Her depression had not magically vanished, nor had her insecurity been a sign that she lacked talent. She performed beautifully, then asked for tea. Noor brought it without a speech. Esteem could be expressed through a cup held out at the right moment.","Minna's cousin arrived with an old poster about the Revolutionary War, part of a different production's archive. They would use its blank reverse for the new cue sheet. The house carried a host of such remnants. It could expand a collection forever if someone did not eventually recycle the paper. Minna conceded that fame had given her too many boxes.","A rash of applause greeted the first full scene. Noor laughed at the phrase when Oliver used it, then asked him to remember that rash also had a literal meaning for someone who had been uncomfortable all week. He apologized cheerfully. There was no stand-off over the small correction. The house's pervasive habit was to talk, not collect grievances in silence.","With the brace repaired, the carpenter signed his work and asked the stage crew to inspect the route. The instruction was traceable to one responsible person. Readiness did not mean everyone claiming confidence at once. The crew checked, agreed, and let the audience in.","Cassian stood beside Mara. \"Honour demands that I rescue the pirate.\"","\"Try not to incur dishonor by tripping over the veil.\"","He took her hand. In front of them, the curtain opened onto a ship sturdy enough to carry the afternoon's absurdities."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1070,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0266\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：The Liberty House Players</div><h1>The Ship That Needed a Brace</h1><p id=\"chapter-0266-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1270\" data-words=\"1270\">carpenter</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3404\" data-words=\"3404,3245\">found</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4951\" data-words=\"4951\">loose</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1076\" data-words=\"1076\">brace</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-923\" data-words=\"923\">beneath</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7494\" data-words=\"7494\">ship</a>. <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3847\" data-words=\"3847\">He</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1941\" data-words=\"1941\">could</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6974\" data-words=\"6974\">repair</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4568\" data-words=\"4568\">it</a>, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1186\" data-words=\"1186\">but</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5566\" data-words=\"5566\">not</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9207\" data-words=\"9207\">while</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6075\" data-words=\"6075\">people</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7896\" data-words=\"7896\">stood</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5700\" data-words=\"5700\">on</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6206\" data-words=\"6206\">platform</a>.
